## 🆕 SECTION 84: FRESH PAIR - PUMP INDICATORS & TRADINGVIEW SCRIPTS (Nov 19, 2026)

When evaluating fresh pairs for potential pump activity, it is essential to use a combination of on-chain data, behavioral patterns, and technical indicators. Pumps often occur due to coordinated buying, liquidity manipulation, or social media hype, making it crucial to identify early signs before a potential surge in price. Several pump indicators are commonly used to detect such activity, and TradingView scripts can be employed to automate and streamline this process.

One of the most commonly used indicators is the **Volume Profile**, which helps identify key support and resistance levels based on historical volume data. High volume at specific price levels can indicate areas where significant buying or selling has occurred in the past, which may repeat during a pump. Another indicator is the **On-Balance Volume (OBV)**, which tracks the flow of volume in relation to price movement. A rising OBV can signal increasing buying pressure, potentially leading to a price increase.

The **Moving Average Convergence Divergence (MACD)** is also a popular tool for analyzing momentum. A divergence between the price and MACD can indicate a potential reversal or continuation of a trend. Additionally, the **Relative Strength Index (RSI)** can help determine overbought or oversold conditions. A sudden spike in RSI above 70 may signal a potential reversal, while a drop below 30 may indicate a buying opportunity.

TradingView scripts can be customized to monitor and highlight specific pump indicators in real-time. These scripts can be programmed to alert traders when

## Cryptomarket Indicator

### Module 1: Relative Strength Leaderboard
**Features:**
- Live 15-coin relative-strength leaderboard
- Identifies emerging coins

The relative-strength leaderboard is a dynamic tool that evaluates the performance of top memecoins based on their relative strength compared to a benchmark index or a group of peer coins. The leaderboard updates in real time and highlights coins that are showing stronger performance in terms of price appreciation and volume surge. This feature helps traders identify potential breakout candidates or coins that may be in the early stages of a pump. The relative strength metric is calculated as the ratio of a coin's price movement to the average price movement of the group, which helps filter out noise and focus on coins that are outperforming their peers. Emerging coins are typically those that show a sharp increase in both price and volume, often indicating a strong buyer interest or a catalyst that has triggered increased attention from the market.

### Module 2: Pump & Dump Warning
**Features:**
- Coin-level pump and dump warning system
- Works on any memecoin perpetual you put it on

The pump and dump warning system is designed to detect and alert traders to potential pump and dump patterns in memecoin perpetual contracts. This system is essential for traders looking to avoid the risks associated with sudden price surges followed by sharp corrections, which are common in the memecoin market. The system uses several detection layers to analyze market conditions and provide warnings based on different stages of a potential pump or dump.

**Detection layers:**

**1. Volume Anomaly Detection**
- Volume Z-Score computed against 20-bar rolling mean and standard deviation
- "Volume Explosion" = Volume Z ≥ user-configurable threshold (default 3.0σ)
- Volume Explosion combined with price action classifies into:
  - 🚀 PUMP INITIATING = Volume Explosion + bar % gain ≥ pump threshold (illustrative: 5%)
  - 🐋 ACCUMULATION = Volume Explosion + flat or down price action (smart-money loading - early stage)
  - ⚠️ LATE STAGE PUMP = Volume Explosion + cumulative price gain already exceeds late-stage threshold (illustrative: 30%) (danger zone - exit setup)

The volume anomaly detection layer is a critical component of the pump and dump warning system. It uses the Z-Score to measure how far the current volume is from the average volume over a 20-bar period. A Z-Score of 3.0σ or higher indicates a significant deviation from the norm, which could be a sign of increased trading activity. When combined with price action, this volume explosion can be classified into different stages of a potential pump or dump. For example, if a coin experiences a volume explosion and the price bar has a gain of at least 5%, it may signal the initiation of a pump. Conversely, if the volume is exploding but the price is flat or declining, it could indicate accumulation, where smart money is buying before a potential pump. However, if the cumulative price gain has already exceeded 30%, it may be a sign of a late-stage pump, where the price is likely to retrace significantly.

**2. Relative Volume vs Sector Context**
- Fetches MEME.C volume and computes its Z-score
- Each pump/dump label tagged with context:
  - (SECTOR) = MEME.C volume is also surging (sector-wide move - broader signal, lower individual alpha)
  - (COIN) = MEME.C volume is flat

An exit ladder typically includes multiple levels: a stop loss, a target profit, and intermediate exit points for partial exits. For example, a trader might set a stop loss at 15% below entry, a target profit at 30% above entry, and intermediate exits at 10% and 20% gains. This approach ensures that the trader remains objective and follows a predefined strategy, regardless of market volatility. Failing to set an exit plan can lead to holding onto losing positions for too long or exiting winning positions too early, both of which can erode overall returns.

## 🆕 SECTION 86: PUMP.FUN BONDING CURVE MECHANICS & GRADUATION STRATEGY (Nov 19, 2026)

Pump.fun operates on a bonding curve mechanism that defines how new tokens are issued and priced as users buy into the platform. This mechanism is critical for both the initial token price and the potential for price volatility. A bonding curve is essentially a mathematical function that maps the total supply of tokens to the price at which new tokens are sold. The curve can be linear, exponential, or logarithmic, depending on the design of the platform. On Pump.fun, the bonding curve is typically designed to increase the price of new tokens rapidly as more tokens are bought, creating a potentially explosive price movement.

The bonding curve is usually initialized with a fixed supply of tokens at a given price. As users purchase tokens, the price is determined by the bonding curve function, which can be described as:

$$ P = f(S) $$

Where $ P $ is the price of a new token and $ S $ is the total supply of tokens sold so far. The function $ f(S) $ is designed to increase the price as more tokens are sold, which can lead to significant price increases for early buyers and potential losses for later buyers if the curve is not managed properly.

The graduation strategy on Pump.fun involves a series of steps that allow the token to move from the bonding curve to a more traditional market structure. This typically includes:

1. **Initial Sale on Bonding Curve:** The token is initially sold on the bonding curve, with the price determined by the curve function.
2. **Graduation to Open Market:** Once a certain threshold of tokens has been sold, the token is moved to an open market, where it can be traded on decentralized exchanges (DEXs

## Bonding Curve Fundamentals

### How It Works
- **Fixed supply:** 1 billion tokens per token
- **Curve allocation:** 800 million sold along constant product bonding curve
- **Contract role:** Contract is counterparty for every trade (~1% fee)
- **Price dynamics:** Buying pushes price up; selling pushes it down

The mechanism of a bonding curve is based on a mathematical relationship between the price of the token and the total supply. When a user buys tokens, the contract sells them at a price that increases with each purchase. This ensures that the first buyer pays a lower price, while subsequent buyers pay progressively higher prices. Similarly, when a user sells tokens, the price decreases, allowing the seller to get a better price than the next seller. The contract acts as the counterparty for every trade, which means it always buys or sells tokens at the current price. This design ensures that the market is always liquid and that the price is determined purely by supply and demand, with the contract absorbing the fee (~1% of the trade value) as a mechanism to fund the ecosystem.

For example, if the bonding curve starts with a price of $0.01 per token and the first buyer purchases 10,000 tokens, the price would increase based on the formula of the curve, which could be a square root or logarithmic curve. This increase in price ensures that the token’s supply is distributed in a way that reflects market demand.

### Graduation Threshold
- **Standard threshold:** ~85 SOL reserves accumulate (~$44K-$69K market cap, varies with SOL price)
- **Graduation destination:** Liquidity auto-deposits to PumpSwap (own DEX, launched March 2025)
- **Migration:** Raydium migration ended; all graduates go to PumpSwap

The graduation threshold is a critical point in the lifecycle of a memecoin, where the token transitions from being traded on the bonding curve to being listed on a decentralized exchange (DEX). The threshold is typically defined by the amount of SOL (Solana's native token) reserves that the bonding curve accumulates. When the reserves reach ~85 SOL, the token is considered to have graduated and is automatically deposited into PumpSwap, the DEX that was launched in March 2025. This transition is important because it allows the token to be traded on a broader market, increasing its visibility and potentially its value.

Before March 2025, tokens that met the graduation threshold were migrated to Raydium, another DEX. However, this migration has since ended, and all new graduates are now directed to PumpSwap. The graduation process is automatic once the threshold is met, and it is designed to ensure that only tokens with sufficient liquidity and market interest are listed on the DEX.

### Graduation Rate Reality
- **Rate:** Only 1-3% of new launches graduate (Dune data)
- **Reality:** Rest are consumed by bonding curve fees

The reality of graduation rates for memecoins is quite low, with only 1-3% of new launches successfully graduating to PumpSwap. This is largely due to the high fees associated with the bonding curve. The contract takes a small percentage (~1%) of each trade as a

## Graduation Timing Strategy

### Pre-Graduation Exit Signal

**Important insight from research:**
> "Selling just before graduation yields more SOL than selling immediately after, which creates a strong incentive for early large holders to dump before graduation."

**Why:**
- **Liquidity discontinuity at migration:** When a token graduates from one market or ecosystem to another (e.g., from a centralized exchange to a decentralized one), synthetic liquidity that was artificially maintained before graduation often disappears. This creates a sudden drop in available liquidity, which can cause price slippage and volatility.
- **Marginal price is continuous when token migrates:** Despite the discontinuity in liquidity, the price of the token at the point of migration is typically maintained as a continuous value. This means that the price doesn't jump abruptly, but the underlying market structure changes.
- **But post-graduation pool is shallower (synthetic liquidity disappears):** After migration, the token is no longer supported by the same synthetic liquidity mechanisms. This makes the market more susceptible to price swings, especially if large holders exit.
- **Early stakeholders can realize more SOL by exiting before graduation:** Because of the shallow post-graduation liquidity, early large holders have a strong incentive to sell their tokens before the migration occurs. This allows them

## Bonding Curve Progress Monitoring

### Progress Indicator
**What it measures:**
- Movement toward launchpad graduation condition
- NOT a token's probability of success
- NOT proof of completed migration

The progress indicator serves as a quantitative measure of how close a token is to fulfilling the conditions required for graduation from a launchpad, typically involving the completion of a bonding curve and the transfer of liquidity to a specific decentralized exchange (DEX) such as PumpSwap. This metric is derived from the bonding curve's current state, which is determined by the amount of liquidity locked, the total supply of the token, and the number of transactions that have occurred. However, it is critical to understand that this indicator does not reflect the likelihood of a token's long-term success or its ability to maintain value post-launch. It also does not serve as evidence that the migration process has been completed. For example, if a token has reached 99% of its bonding curve's target liquidity, the progress indicator would reflect this, but it would not confirm that the token has successfully moved to the target pool or that the migration is complete.

### Card Signals
**Read stages and card signals together:**
- Curve progress measures movement toward graduation condition
- Almost bonded is a scanner grouping, not proof of completed migration
- Pump.fun documents graduation to PumpSwap (NOT Raydium)
- Do not assume every pump.fun token migrates to Raydium
- Distinguish

# 🆕 SECTION 89: NARRATIVE TRACKING & ROTATION STRATEGY (Nov 19, 2026)

Narrative tracking and rotation strategy is a sophisticated method used to identify and capitalize on the evolving stories that drive meme coin movements. This approach involves monitoring the thematic narratives surrounding a particular coin

## What is a Narrative?

**Definition:**
> "A narrative is the shared story behind a group of meme coins: a mascot, a person, a joke format, or piece of news that many separate launches borrow."

A narrative in the context of meme coins refers to the overarching theme or conceptual framework that unites multiple tokens or projects within the meme coin ecosystem. This narrative can be a visual symbol, a cultural reference, a piece of internet humor, or a specific event that resonates with a community. It serves as the foundation for community engagement, marketing, and even speculative value. The narrative is often more important than the technical or functional aspects of the token itself, as it drives emotional and social investment from the community.

**Examples:**
- Dog coins (dogs

## Narrative Rotation Pattern

### How It Works
> "When one token in a theme draws volume, copycats and related launches often follow. When interest fades, the whole group can lose liquidity together."

The narrative rotation pattern is a recurring phenomenon in the memecoin and altcoin space, driven by the cyclical nature of investor attention and the way capital flows in response to hype and novelty. This pattern is rooted in the psychological tendency of traders and investors to follow trends, especially in highly speculative markets where sentiment and social media influence play a dominant role. When one token within a particular theme gains traction—whether through viral marketing, a celebrity endorsement, or a unique feature—it often acts as a catalyst that draws attention and liquidity to the entire theme. This increased liquidity can then be leveraged by project teams or market participants to launch copycat tokens or related projects that aim to capitalize on the same narrative.

## Tracking Narrative-Level Performance

### Metrics to Track

**Per narrative:**

| Metric | Meaning |
|--------|---------|
| **Narrative Volume Share** | Narrative's share of all traded volume over window |
| **Narrative Heat** | Log of short-window share over long-window share; >0 means gaining attention |
| **Narrative Return** | Trailing liquidity-weighted return of narrative, deaths at -100% |
| **Launch Saturation** | Copycat launches now vs narrative's usual pace; >2 means crowded |

The **Narrative Volume Share** is a key indicator of the dominance of a particular narrative in the memecoin trading space. It measures the proportion of total traded volume that is attributed to tokens associated with that narrative over a specified time window, such as a week or a month. A higher volume share indicates that the narrative is more actively traded, potentially due to increased interest, marketing efforts, or new developments. For example, if a narrative has a volume share of 35% over a one-week period, it means that 35% of all memec

## Narrative Strategies

### Strategy 1: Narrative Rotation (Own Leaders)
**Thesis:** Own the leaders of narratives gaining share of volume  
**Need:** Names of tokens in each narrative  
**Variants:** 8  

**Execution:**  
1. **Identify narratives gaining volume share** – This involves tracking on-chain data, social media sentiment, and trading volume across different memecoins. The goal is to detect narratives that are becoming increasingly popular and are attracting significant trading activity. For example, a narrative around AI integration in blockchain or a new DeFi protocol may start to show increased volume as more traders and investors begin to engage with it.  
2. **Find the leaders (best performers in narrative)** – Once a narrative is identified, the next step is to determine which tokens within that narrative are leading the charge in terms of price performance and volume. These leaders are often the first to experience significant price appreciation and may be the ones that attract the most attention from the broader market. This can be done by analyzing price charts, volume trends, and social media mentions.  
3. **Buy leaders before they rotate to copycats** – The key here is timing. Once a narrative gains traction, it often leads to the emergence of copycat tokens that mimic the original idea. These copycats may initially follow the trend of the original token but eventually diverge in performance. By buying the original leaders before the narrative becomes saturated with copycats, traders can capture the initial price appreciation before the market becomes crowded.  
4. **Exit when narrative becomes crowded** – As more tokens enter a narrative, the market becomes saturated, and the performance of individual tokens may begin to slow. At this point, it's important to exit the position to avoid potential losses. This can be signaled by increased volatility, reduced price movement, or a significant rise in the number of tokens associated with the same narrative.

## Narrative Tracking Tools

### MemeAssist
**Features:**
- 90 narrative themes tracked
- Ten sections (dogs, cats, AI, politics, etc.)
- Each card shows top 3 tokens by health score
- Dedicated page per theme

**How it works:**
- Measures narratives from saved reports: The system parses and analyzes historical and current memecoins reports, identifying and categorizing recurring narrative themes. This helps in understanding the underlying sentiment and direction of memecoins communities.
- Tokens matched by whole-word vocabularies on name and ticker: This ensures that each memecoin is accurately categorized under the relevant narrative based on its name and ticker symbol. For example, a token named "DogeCoin" would be associated with the "dogs" theme.
- Exclusions for known false positives: The system is programmed to exclude tokens that are known to be false positives, such as those with similar names or ticker symbols that do not align with the narrative themes.
- Wrapped, staked, infrastructure assets left out: These types of assets are excluded to maintain the focus on pure memecoins and avoid diluting the narrative analysis with other asset types.
- For every narrative: matched tokens, average health score, low rug-risk share: Each narrative theme provides a list of matched tokens, their average health score, and the share of tokens with low rug-risk. This helps in identifying which memecoins are not only aligned with the narrative but also are less likely to be rug pulls.

The health score is calculated based on a combination of factors including social media engagement, market performance, and community sentiment. A token with a higher health score is considered more stable and less likely to be a rug pull. The low rug-risk share indicates the proportion of tokens within a narrative that are deemed safe from rug pulls. This information is crucial for traders looking to invest in memecoins that are not only aligned with a strong narrative but also have a lower risk of being scammed.

## Complete Fresh Pair Trading System

### PHASE 1: DETECTION (Every 60 Seconds)
- [ ] Pull new pairs from DexScreener  
  DexScreener is used to identify newly listed tokens on decentralized exchanges. These tokens are typically in the early stages of their lifecycle, making them candidates for rapid price movements. The data is pulled every 60 seconds to ensure the system remains up-to-date with the latest listings.
- [ ] Pull fresh pump.fun migrations to PumpSwap/Raydium  
  pump.fun is a platform used to launch new memecoins, often through automated pumps and dumps. Migrations from pump.fun to PumpSwap or Raydium indicate that a token has moved from its initial launch platform to a more established decentralized exchange, which can be a signal of increased interest or a potential price surge.
- [ ] Save one row to candidates.json for each pair  
  Each newly detected pair is stored in a JSON file under the key "candidates.json" to be analyzed in subsequent phases.
  - Token address  
    The unique identifier of the token on the blockchain.
  - Age in minutes  
    The time since the token was listed or launched, which helps assess its maturity and potential for price action.
  - Liquidity  
    The amount of tokens available for trading, which is a critical factor in determining the stability of a token's price.
  - Volume 5m  
    The trading volume over the last 5 minutes, which can indicate immediate interest or activity in the token.
  - Unique buyers 5m  
    The number of unique addresses that have bought the token in the last 5 minutes, which can suggest the level of participation and potential for a price increase.
  - Dev wallet  
    The wallet address associated with the developers or creators of the token, which can be a red flag if it holds a large portion of the supply.
  - Top 10 holders %  
    The percentage of the token's supply held by the top 10 addresses, which can indicate centralization or potential for price manipulation.
  - Link  
    A link to the token’s listing on a decentralized exchange or other relevant platform.
  - **Bonding curve progress**  
    The progress of the token through its bonding curve, which is a mechanism that determines the price based on supply. Tokens nearing the graduation threshold (often around 85 SOL) may be close to completing their bonding curve and could experience a price surge.
  - **Social sentiment score**  
    A metric derived from social media activity, such as mentions on Twitter/X, which reflects the overall sentiment towards the token.
  - **Narrative theme**  
    The underlying theme or story associated with the token, such as "Dog," "Cat," "AI," or "Politics," which can influence investor behavior and price action.

### PHASE 2: ANALYSIS (Before Entry)
- [ ] Run contract through RugCheck or Token Sniffer (1 minute)  
  Tools like RugCheck and Token Sniffer are used to analyze the smart contract of the token to identify potential risks such as rug pulls, hidden fees, or other vulnerabilities. These checks are performed within 1 minute to ensure efficiency.
- [ ] Check top holder distribution on block explorer (1 minute)  
  This step involves examining the distribution of the token's supply among the top holders on a block explorer. A highly concentrated distribution may indicate a higher risk of price manipulation.
- [ ] Search token name on Twitter/X and assess engagement quality (1 minute)  
  Engagement on social media platforms like Twitter/X is a key indicator of the token's popularity and potential for price movement. High-quality engagement, such as retweets, likes, and comments, can be a positive sign.
- [ ] Review chart on DexScreener for healthy price action (1 minute)  
  The price chart is reviewed to identify any signs of abnormal or unhealthy price action, such as sudden spikes or dips that may indicate market manipulation.
- [ ] Check if Telegram/Discord community is active and organic (1 minute)  
  An active and organic community on platforms like Telegram or Discord can indicate genuine interest in the token and may be a sign of potential price movement.
- [ ] Score: 70-77 / 78-84 / 85-92  
  Tokens are scored based on various factors, such as liquidity, volume, and social sentiment. A score of 70-77 indicates a lower risk, 78-84 is moderate, and 85-92 is high risk with higher potential for price movement.
- [ ] Check liquidity: Pool depth vs position size (should be ≤2%)  
  The pool depth refers to the amount of liquidity available in the trading pool, and the position size should not exceed 2% of the pool to avoid large price

## Advanced Strategies

### Strategy 1: Pre-Graduation Exit  
**Thesis:** Sell just before graduation for more SOL  
**Execution:**  
- Monitor bonding curve progress: Graduation typically refers to a point where a memecoin project reaches a specific token supply or market cap threshold, often triggering a shift in token mechanics (e.g., transitioning from a bonding curve to a fixed supply). Tracking the bonding curve's progress is crucial because the price often reacts predictably as this threshold approaches.  
- Exit 5-10% before graduation threshold: This allows traders to secure profits before the potential price decline that often follows graduation. The exact percentage can vary depending on the specific project and market conditions, but 5-10% is a commonly observed range.  
- Don't wait for migration: Migration refers to the process of transitioning from one blockchain or token structure to another, which can cause temporary price volatility. Exiting before this event reduces exposure to unpredictable price swings.  
- Price action often weakens as graduation approaches: This is due to the mechanics of bonding curves, which can experience a drop in demand as the token supply nears its cap, leading to a price decline. Traders who recognize this pattern can time their exits more effectively.  
**Mechanisms:** The pre-graduation exit works by leveraging the predictable behavior of bonding curves. As the token supply approaches its cap, the marginal cost of buying new tokens increases, reducing demand and causing a price decline. By exiting early, traders can lock in gains before this drop occurs.

## Common Mistakes to Avoid

**Problem:** Selling just before graduation yields more SOL

Traders often believe that waiting until this event will result in a significant price increase, but in reality, the price may already be factoring in this expectation. As a result, selling at the last minute may not yield the expected returns, especially if the token experiences a post-graduation price drop due to market saturation or realization of overvaluation.

## 🆕 SECTION 91: ADVANCED ON-CHAIN ANALYTICS FOR FRESH PAIRS (Nov 19, 2026)

On-chain analytics play a critical role in evaluating the potential of fresh memecoins, as they provide a transparent and quantifiable way to assess market behavior, liquidity dynamics, and potential risks. These analytics are particularly useful in detecting early-stage patterns that may not be visible through traditional market analysis alone. ### Detection: Pulling New Pairs and Tracking Bonding Curve Progress

Fresh memecoins are typically listed on decentralized exchanges (DEXs) with limited liquidity, often through bonding curves or liquidity pools. The process of pulling new pairs every 60 seconds ensures that traders can quickly identify and analyze emerging tokens. DexScreener and Pump.fun King of Hill are tools that aggregate and display this data in real-time, providing insights into the token's initial trading volume, price movement, and on-chain activity.

Bonding curve progress refers to the rate at which liquidity is being added to the pool through the bonding curve mechanism. This is critical because it directly affects the token's price and market cap. For example, if a bonding curve has 10,000 tokens available and 2,000 have been sold, the price will rise as more liquidity is locked in. This can be tracked using on-chain analytics tools that show the percentage of tokens allocated and the rate of token sales. If a token has a high bonding curve progress (e.g., 80% of tokens sold), it may indicate strong initial demand or a potential risk of rapid price volatility.

### Analysis: 8-Step Rug Check and Narrative Saturation

## Why Advanced On-Chain Matters

> "A single entity can control dozens of wallets, spread supply across fresh addresses, and make a token look far more decentralized than it really is."

**Standard rug checks miss:**
- **Wallet clusters (connected wallets controlled by same entity):** This refers to a group of wallets that, although appearing distinct on the blockchain, are actually controlled by the same individual or organization. These wallets may be linked through shared transaction patterns, common addresses used in multi-signature setups, or behavioral similarities. For example, if multiple wallets send the same amount of tokens to the same address in the same block, this could indicate coordination. Standard rug checks that only look at wallet addresses in isolation may miss this clustering, especially

## Wallet Clustering Detection

### What It Is
> "Bubblemaps displays holders as bubbles. The size of each bubble reflects the amount of supply held, and the links between bubbles show transfers between wallets."

**What it Is:**  
Wallet clustering detection is a technique used in blockchain analysis to identify groups of wallets that are connected through transfers or other interactions. This process helps uncover patterns in how token supply is distributed across wallets and can be used to detect anomalies that may indicate manipulation, insider activity, or coordinated behavior.

**What it reveals:**  
- **Connected wallets grouped visually:** By mapping transfers and interactions, wallet clustering allows users to see which wallets are linked, providing a visual representation of how tokens are moving through the network.  
- **Clusters holding large share of supply:** When a group of connected wallets collectively holds a significant portion of the total supply, it may indicate a concentration of power or control within a small set of entities.  
- **Transfer patterns between wallets:** By analyzing how tokens move between wallets, users can detect recurring patterns or unusual activity that may suggest coordinated trading or insider trading.  
- **Sybil attack patterns (one entity, many wallets):** In cases where a single entity operates multiple wallets, these wallets may appear as separate entities in the network. However, if they are connected through transfers or common funding sources, clustering detection can identify such patterns and flag them as potential sybil attacks.

**Red flags:**  
- **Cluster of connected wallets holding large supply:** If a group of wallets connected through transfers holds a large portion of the total supply, it may indicate that a small group of entities controls a significant share of the token. This can be a red flag for market manipulation or centralization of control.  
- **Multiple fresh wallets funded within preceding 10 days:** When many wallets are newly created and funded within a short time frame, it may indicate a coordinated effort to distribute tokens across multiple wallets, potentially to obscure ownership or manipulate the market.  
- **Wallets connected through common funding sources:** If multiple wallets are connected through the same funding source, such as a centralized exchange or a single wallet address, it may suggest that the same entity controls these wallets.  
- **60% of top holders = fresh wallets with no prior activity:** If a large percentage of the top holders are fresh wallets with no prior activity, it could indicate a coordinated distribution or a potential wash trade. This is particularly concerning in memeco

This score is a quantitative measure that reflects the frequency with which a wallet has engaged in co-buy events, which can be used to infer the wallet's potential influence or coordination with others.

## Correlation Analysis

### Understanding Correlation

**Correlation coefficient ranges from +1 to -1:**
- **+1:** Perfect positive correlation (both move together)
- **0:** No correlation (independent movements)
- **-1:** Perfect negative correlation (opposite movements)

The correlation coefficient is a statistical measure that quantifies the degree to which two assets move in relation to each other. A correlation of +1 means the assets move in perfect unison, while a correlation of -1 means they move in exact opposition. A value of 0 implies no linear relationship. This measure is crucial in portfolio management, as it helps investors understand how different assets interact and how diversification can reduce risk.

**Diversification formula:**
```
diversification benefit = (weighted average of the volatilities) − (portfolio volatility)
```

The diversification benefit is a measure of how much risk is reduced by combining different assets in a portfolio. It is calculated by subtracting the portfolio's overall volatility from the weighted average of the individual volatilities of the assets. A higher diversification benefit indicates a more effective portfolio in terms of risk reduction. For example, if the weighted average volatility of individual assets is 20% and the portfolio volatility is 12%, the diversification benefit would be 8%, indicating

## Portfolio Diversification Rules

### Rule #1: Never Risk More Than 2-5% Per Position  
> "Position size ≤5% of portfolio"

**Why:**  
- Survive 10 consecutive losses with 50-80% capital intact: By limiting each position to 2-5% of the total portfolio, a trader can endure a sequence of 10 losing trades (each with a 10% loss) while retaining between 50% and 80% of the original capital. This ensures that the trader is not wiped out by a single asset’s performance and maintains the ability to continue trading.
- Avoid "dead money" tied up indefinitely: Position sizing prevents the allocation of too much capital to a single asset that may become untradable or illiquid. This is especially important in memecoin markets, where projects can become obsolete or delisted rapidly.
- Preserve capital for fresh opportunities: By keeping positions small, the trader maintains flexibility and liquidity to capitalize on new trading opportunities as they arise. For example, if a trader has $100,000 in portfolio capital, a 5% position size would allow for a $5,000 investment per trade, leaving the rest of the capital available for other trades or to buffer against volatility.

**Mechanism:**  
This rule is implemented through a simple percentage-based position sizing formula. If a trader has a total portfolio of $100,000 and adheres to a 5% position size rule, each trade is limited to $5,000. This ensures that no single trade can consume more than a small fraction of the total capital, even if it moves against the trader.

**Edge Cases:**  
- In highly volatile markets, even small position sizes can lead to large drawdowns. Therefore, traders may consider reducing their position size further during periods of high volatility or uncertainty.
- For high-probability, low-risk trades, the position size can be increased slightly within the 2-5% range. However, this should

For example, if the portfolio includes multiple memecoins with high correlation due to similar market drivers, reducing exposure to one or more of them can lower overall risk.

## Diversification Benefits

### True Diversification Requires:

**1. Assets that respond differently to same stress event**  
Diversification is most effective when the assets in a portfolio react differently to the same market stressor. This is because the varying responses can mitigate overall portfolio risk. For example, memecoins and Bitcoin are driven by different factors: memecoins are often influenced by social media trends, internet culture, and speculative hype, while Bitcoin is influenced by macroeconomic factors, institutional adoption, and regulatory developments. When a systemic event like a global economic downturn occurs, Bitcoin may decline due to risk-off behavior, whereas memecoins may either remain stable or even increase in value if the event triggers a surge in speculative trading.

Similarly, combining memecoins with stablecoins can provide a yield-based diversification benefit. Stablecoins, by design, maintain a stable value pegged to fiat currencies, and some stablecoins offer yield through staking or lending mechanisms. In a portfolio that includes both memecoins and stablecoins, the volatile memecoins can be balanced by the relatively stable and yield-generating stablecoins. This combination allows for potential upside from memecoins while providing a floor of

## Portfolio Construction Process

### Step 1: Plan Your Allocation
- Choose pairs with low or negative correlation: This is a foundational principle in portfolio construction, especially in a volatile market like memecoin trading. By selecting assets (or memecoins) that tend to move in opposite directions or show minimal co-movement, investors can reduce overall portfolio volatility. For example, if memecoin A tends to rise when memecoin B falls, and vice versa, their inclusion in a portfolio can act as a hedge against one another.
- Size each position so combined risk stays inside account rules: This involves using risk management techniques to ensure that no single position or group of positions exceeds predefined risk thresholds. This is often done using Value at Risk (VaR) models or volatility-based sizing. For example, if a portfolio has a maximum allowable daily drawdown of 2%, the size of each position must be adjusted so that the worst-case loss does not exceed this threshold. The exact sizing method can depend on the investor's risk appetite and the volatility of the assets involved.
- Keep part of balance in cash as buffer: Maintaining liquidity in the form of cash or highly liquid assets is essential for managing unexpected market movements or opportunities. This buffer can be used for rebalancing, taking advantage of temporary price dips, or covering margin requirements. The proportion of cash can vary depending on the volatility of the market and the investor’s strategy, but a common range is between 10% and 30% of

## Limit Order Strategies

### What Are Limit Orders

A **limit order** is an instruction to buy or sell a token at a specified price or better. Unlike market orders, which execute immediately at the current price, limit orders allow traders to set a specific price threshold for entry or exit. This provides greater control over the cost basis or exit price, though it comes with the risk of the order not being filled if the market does not reach the specified price.

**Benefits:**
- More suitable for plans with clear price zones: Limit orders are ideal for traders who have a well-defined price range in which they believe a token will trade. This is particularly useful when the trader has a hypothesis about support/resistance levels or other price structures.
- Wait for support level pullbacks: Traders can use limit orders to enter positions when a token pulls back to a known support level, which is often a favorable entry point for long positions.
- Buy on retests after breakouts: After a breakout from a key level, traders can use limit orders to buy on retests of the breakout level, which can indicate continued strength or a potential reversal.
- Sell in batches at resistance zones: When a token approaches a resistance level, traders can use limit orders to sell in batches, locking in profits at predefined levels.
- Use preset prices to reduce emotional operations: By setting specific entry and exit points in advance, traders can minimize the impact of emotions on their decisions, leading to more disciplined and consistent trading.

**How to use:**
```
Write in advance:
- Entry price range: Define the price range in which you are willing to enter the trade. This can be a single price or a range.
- Whether partial fills are allowed: Decide whether you want the order to be filled in parts if the price reaches the limit, or if you want it to be filled only in full.
- Whether to chase if not filled: Determine if you are willing to adjust the price of the limit order if it is not filled within a certain timeframe.
- After how long without filling to cancel: Set a time limit for how long the limit order should remain active if it is not filled. This helps avoid holding onto orders that are unlikely to be executed.
```

## Batch Trading Strategies

### Batch Entry

**What it is:**
- Change price point to price range
- Place multiple tiers at different prices
- If price only has shallow pullback = partial fills
- If price continues lower = fill more

**Important:**
> "Batching is not unconditionally safer. Without total risk constraints, batch buying can easily evolve into buying more as it falls."

Batch entry is a strategy where traders aim to accumulate a position over a defined price range by placing orders at multiple price levels. This approach allows for partial fills when the price only experiences a shallow pullback and full fills if the price continues to decline. The rationale behind this method is to reduce the impact of market volatility on entry price and to avoid the risk of missing a potential opportunity due to a single entry point. However, it is crucial to note that this strategy is not inherently risk-free. If a trader does not define a total loss limit upfront, they might end up increasing their position size repeatedly as the price continues to fall, leading to a larger downside exposure than initially intended.

**Correct approach:**
1. First determine TOTAL loss limit
2. Then allocate each tier's position
3. After each tier fills, recalculate overall stop loss and average cost
4. Ensure worst-case total loss is still within planned range

To implement batch entry effectively, the trader must first establish a total loss limit. This limit is the maximum amount of capital the trader is willing to risk on the trade. Once this limit is set, the trader can allocate the position size for each tier based on the total loss limit. For example, if the total loss limit is $1,000, the trader might divide this limit into three tiers, each with a position size of $333.33. After each tier is filled, the trader should recalculate the overall stop loss and average cost. This helps in adjusting the risk exposure dynamically as the price fluctuates. Finally, the trader must ensure that even in the worst-case scenario, the total loss does not exceed the planned range. This ensures that the trader remains disciplined and avoids overexposure.

### Batch Take Profit

**What it is:**
- Scale out as coin moves in

## Advanced Execution Algorithms

### Algorithmic Execution Strategy

**Definition:**
> "An algorithmic execution strategy is a rules-based program that determines how a parent order is split into smaller child orders and then paced, priced or triggered against the market."

**Objectives:**
- **Track a volume curve:** By aligning execution with historical or expected volume patterns, traders aim to avoid large market impact and reduce slippage. This is particularly important in highly liquid assets where volume trends can be reliably predicted.
- **Minimize cost against reference price:** This involves calculating the difference between the execution price and a reference price (e.g., a benchmark index or the arrival price) to ensure that the total cost of the trade is as low as possible.
- **Hold position relative to best bid/offer:** This objective focuses on maintaining a position that is as close as possible to the best bid or offer price, ensuring that the trader can exit the position with minimal slippage.
- **Trade into scheduled market event:** Traders may want to adjust their execution strategy in anticipation of events like earnings reports, economic data releases, or market open/close auctions, which can cause significant price movements.

### Algorithm Families

| **Schedule-driven (TWAP, VWAP)** | Track time/volume benchmark | Time or volume curve | Low-urgency orders in liquid names | Relies on patterns holding | These algorithms are effective in liquid markets where historical patterns are consistent. For example, a trader using TWAP (Time Weighted Average Price) may execute trades at regular intervals to match the average price over a specific time period. However, if market conditions change unexpectedly, such as a sudden news event, the effectiveness of the strategy may be compromised. |
| **Participation-driven (POV)** | Stay proportional to volume/liquidity | Real-time volume | Orders that must scale with liquidity | Quiet session leaves order unfinished | POV (Percentage of Volume) strategies ensure that the trader's order size is proportional to the volume traded in the market. This is useful in scenarios where the trader wants to avoid being the largest participant in the market. However, during periods of low liquidity, the algorithm may not be able to execute the full order, leaving it partially filled. |
| **Cost-driven (Implementation Shortfall)** | Minimize gap between arrival and execution | Price vs. arrival price | Orders where waiting cost outweighs footprint | Larger, front-loaded footprint | This strategy aims to minimize the difference between the price at which the order was initially placed (arrival price) and the actual execution price. It is particularly useful for orders where the cost of waiting for a better price is higher than the potential slippage from immediate execution. However, this can lead to larger, front-loaded trades that may impact the market more significantly. |
| **Price-driven (pegging, limit-tracking)** | Hold price relative to book reference | Order book reference | Orders where price control matters | Fills may be slow or incomplete | Price-driven strategies involve maintaining a position relative to the best bid or offer price. This is useful for traders who need to control the price at which they enter or exit a position. However, these strategies can be slow to fill, especially in illiquid markets where the best bid or offer may not be updated frequently. |

## Execution Path Options

### Path 1: Next-Open Execution

**When to use:**
- Liquid names with moderate size
- Clean signals at close

**Workflow:**
1. Observe signal at end of day
2. Enter at open on next day
3. Exit by same convention when close-based rules trigger

This execution path relies on the assumption that the market will open with a price that reflects the signal observed at the close of the previous day. It is particularly useful for liquid names where the price is expected to remain relatively stable overnight, and where the signal is strong and clean. The next-open execution strategy capitalizes on the fact that the open can act as a proxy for the closing price, especially in markets with low overnight volatility. The mechanism works by entering the position at the next market open, which is typically the most liquid time of the day. This method avoids the potential for slippage that can occur during the transition between market close and open, especially in volatile markets.

A typical example would be a trader observing a strong close signal on a liquid stock, say with a volume of 100,000 shares, and entering the same size at the next open. If the stock is expected to open within a tight range of the previous close, the trader can expect minimal slippage. However, in cases of significant overnight news or volatility, the next-open price may deviate from the expected range, leading to slippage. Traders should also be prepared to adjust their execution strategy if the next-open price does not align with the signal.

**When to use:**
- Opening prints are noisy
- Size matters more

## Impact Reduction Strategies

### Market Impact

**Problem:**
- Placing overly large order at once may expose intent
- May affect price in thin liquidity pairs

**Solutions:**

**1. Batching**
- Use different validity periods and price tiers
- Helps reduce market impact
- Increases management difficulty

Batching involves breaking down a large order into smaller, discrete trades that are executed at different times or under different conditions. This method reduces the visibility of the overall trade and minimizes the chance of triggering price slippage, especially in low-liquidity markets where a single large trade can disproportionately affect the price. By using different validity periods (e.g., immediate or cancel, fill or kill, or time-in-force parameters) and price tiers (e.g., placing orders at different price levels), traders can distribute their buying or selling pressure across the order book. This strategy is especially effective in memecoin markets, where liquidity can be highly volatile and order books are often shallow. However, the increased complexity of managing multiple

## Execution Best Practices

### Rule #1: Write Plan Before Placing Order

**Trading plans should first define:**
- Trading hypotheses: This includes the rationale behind the trade, such as technical indicators, market sentiment, or fundamental events. The hypothesis should be specific and testable.
- Entry, invalidation points: The entry point is the price level at which the trader will enter the trade. Invalidation points are the levels at which the original hypothesis is no longer valid, and the trade should be exited.
- Stop loss: A predetermined price level at which the trader will exit the trade to limit losses. This should be set based on risk management principles and the volatility of the asset.
- Target levels: The price levels at which the trader expects to exit the trade for profit. These should be based on technical analysis or market expectations.
- Positions: The size of the position to be taken, often determined by the trader’s risk tolerance, account size, and the stop loss level.

**NOT:** Place orders first, then look for reasons — this can lead to impulsive trading and poor decision-making. By writing the plan first, traders can avoid emotional decisions and stick to a disciplined approach.

The importance of a trading plan lies in its ability to remove emotion from the decision-making process. For example, a trader might have a hypothesis that a certain memecoin will rise in value after a

## Execution Workflow

### Pre-Entry

**Step 1: Define Plan**  
- Write down trading hypothesis: This step involves documenting the rationale behind the trade, such as the expected price movement based on market trends, news events, or technical indicators. The hypothesis must be specific and quantifiable.  
- Define entry price range: This is the range of prices at which the trader intends to enter the market. It provides flexibility in case the price fluctuates before the trade is executed.  
- Define invalidation point: The invalidation point is a specific price level that, if reached, would indicate that the original hypothesis is incorrect. This helps in managing risk by setting a clear threshold for when the trade should be reconsidered or exited.  
- Define stop loss: A stop loss is a predetermined price level at which the trade will be closed to prevent further losses. It acts as a risk management tool to limit downside exposure.  
- Define profit targets: Profit targets are the price levels at which the trader intends to exit the trade to realize gains. They are based on the expected price movement and the risk-reward ratio of the trade.

**Step 2: Choose Execution Method**  
- Market order (simple, urgent): A market order executes immediately at the best available price. It is suitable for urgent trades where the exact price is not a concern. However, it may result in slippage, especially in volatile markets.  
- Limit order (price control): A limit order allows the trader to specify the maximum price they are willing to pay (for a buy order) or the minimum price they are willing to accept (for a sell order). This method provides control over the execution price but may not be filled if the price does not reach the specified level.  
- Batch entry (flexibility): Batch entry involves placing multiple orders at different price levels to gradually enter the market.

## Advanced Execution Tools

### Automated Execution

**Features:**
- Trading bots with preset parameters: These bots operate based on predefined rules and strategies, allowing traders to execute trades without manual intervention. Parameters may include price thresholds, time intervals, and risk management settings.
- Automated TP/SL monitoring: Traders can set take-profit (TP) and stop-loss (SL) levels, and the bot will automatically execute these orders when the specified conditions are met. This helps in managing risk and locking in profits.
- Circuit breaker: A safety mechanism that halts trading activity under certain conditions, such as extreme market volatility or predefined price thresholds, to prevent large losses.
- Trade persistence: Ensures that trades are not lost due to network issues or platform downtime. This is particularly important for long-term strategies

## Execution Strategy Comparison

| Strategy | Best For | Pros | Cons |
|----------|----------|------|------|
| **Market Order** | Urgent entries | Immediate fill | Worst price |
| **Limit Order** | Clear price zones | Price control | May not fill |
| **Batch Entry** | Uncertain entry | Flexibility | Complexity |
| **TWAP** | Large orders, liquid pairs | Smooth execution | Time-based |
| **VWAP** | Volume-dependent | Scales with market | May leave order unfinished |
| **Algorithmic** | Complex strategies | Optimized | Requires setup |

## The Complete Fresh Pair Trading System

### PHASE 1: DETECTION (Every 60 Seconds)

**Sources:**
- [ ] Pull new pairs from DexScreener
- [ ] Pull fresh pump.fun migrations to PumpSwap/Raydium
- [ ] Monitor King of the Hill for approaching graduation

**Candidate Data:**
- [ ] Token address
- [ ] Age in minutes
- [ ] Liquidity
- [ ] Volume 5m
- [ ] Unique buyers 5m
- [ ] Dev wallet
- [ ] Top 10 holders %
- [ ] Link
- [ ] **Bonding curve progress**
- [ ] **Social sentiment score**
- [ ] **Narrative theme**
- [ ] **Wallet cluster status**
- [ ] **Smart money convergence**

This phase is crucial for identifying potential memecoins that have the potential to rise in value quickly. DexScreener is a powerful tool for tracking new token listings on various DEXs, providing real-time data on volume, liquidity, and other key metrics. Pump.fun is a popular platform for memecoins, where new tokens are often migrated to PumpSwap or Raydium, which are known for their high-liquidity pools and frequent trading activity. Monitoring King of the Hill helps traders identify tokens that are on the verge of graduating, which is a key event in the lifecycle of a memecoin as it moves to a higher liquidity pool and potentially attracts more attention from the broader market.

### PHASE 2: ANALYSIS (Before Entry - 8 Steps)

**Step 1: Contract Security (1 minute)**
- [ ] Run contract through RugCheck or Token Sniffer
- [ ] Check mint/freeze authority
- [ ] Verify LP tokens (burned or locked with verifiable date)

Contract security is a fundamental aspect of any trading strategy. Tools like RugCheck and Token Sniffer analyze the smart contract code for vulnerabilities, such as rug pulls, which can lead to significant losses. Checking the mint and freeze authority ensures that the token has a clear mechanism for supply control and that the developers cannot arbitrarily freeze the token or manipulate the supply. Verifying if LP tokens are burned or locked with a verifiable date is essential for ensuring that the liquidity is not being manipulated or removed at a later date.

**Step 2: Holder Distribution (1 minute)**
- [ ] Check top holder distribution on block explorer
- [ ] Look for concentration (red flag if >20%)
- [ ] Check for fresh wallet clusters

Holder distribution is a key metric in assessing the risk of a token. A concentration of more than 20% in the top holders can indicate a centralization risk, where a small group of entities controls a significant portion of the supply, potentially leading to market manipulation. Fresh wallet clusters may indicate the presence of bots or whales trying to accumulate large amounts of the token, which can be a warning sign for potential price manipulation or rug pulls.

**Step 3: Social Sentiment (1 minute)**
- [ ] Search token name on Twitter/X
- [ ] Assess engagement quality
- [ ] Check mention volume
- [ ] Score: High/Medium/Low

Social sentiment is a critical factor in memecoin trading, as these tokens often rely on community-driven hype and virality. Assessing engagement quality involves looking at the nature of the interactions—whether they are genuine discussions, promotional content, or spam. Mention volume helps gauge the level of attention the token is receiving, with higher mention volumes often correlating with increased trading activity. Scoring the sentiment as high, medium, or low provides a quick way to categorize the token's potential for growth based on community interest.

**Step 4: Price Action (1 minute)**
- [ ] Review chart on DexScreener
- [ ] Look for healthy price action
- [ ] Check volume patterns

Price action analysis involves reviewing the chart to identify patterns that may indicate future price movements. Healthy price action typically includes consistent volume spikes, which suggest real trading activity rather than artificial inflation. Volume patterns are also important, as they can indicate whether the price movement is supported by actual demand or is a result of manipulation.

**Step 5: Community Check (1 minute)**
- [ ] Check if Telegram/Discord community is active
- [ ] Verify organic growth
- [ ] Look for bot signals

A vibrant community on platforms like Telegram or Discord is a strong indicator of a token's potential. Verifying organic growth helps ensure that the community is not being artificially inflated by bots or spam accounts. Bot signals, such as repetitive messages or suspicious activity, can be a red flag for potential manipulation or rug pulls.

**Step 6: Bonding Curve Progress (1 minute)**
- [ ] Check progress toward graduation
- [ ] If near graduation (<85 SOL threshold), consider exiting
- [ ] If approaching "King of the Hill", consider accumulating

Bonding curve progress is a key metric for memecoins, as it indicates how close the token is to graduating to a higher liquidity pool. If the token is near the graduation threshold, it may be a good time to exit to lock in profits. Conversely, if the token is approaching the "King of the Hill" stage, it may be a good opportunity to accumulate more tokens, as the token is likely to experience a surge in value as it graduates.

**Step 7: Social Sentiment (1 minute)**
- [ ] Check mention volume
- [ ] Check sentiment score
- [ ] Identify narrative theme

This step involves re-evaluating the social sentiment to ensure that the token is still receiving positive attention. Checking the sentiment score again helps confirm that the token's narrative is still resonating with the community. Identifying the narrative theme, such as a dog, cat, AI, politics, etc., helps traders understand the token's positioning within the broader market and its potential for virality.

**Step 8: Narrative Analysis (1 minute)**
- [ ] Identify narrative theme (dog, cat, AI, politics, etc.)
- [ ] Check narrative saturation
- [ ] Identify if leader or copycat

Narrative analysis is crucial for understanding the token's potential for growth. Identifying the narrative theme helps traders assess the token's positioning within the broader market. Checking narrative saturation helps determine whether the token is part of a crowded market or a niche opportunity. Identifying whether the token is a leader or a copycat can help traders decide whether to invest in the token or wait for a more promising opportunity.

**Score:**
- [ ] 70-77: Small position (3%)
- [ ] 78-84: Medium position (5%)
- [ ] 85-92: Large position (6.5%)

The score is based on the cumulative analysis of all eight steps. A score of 70-77 indicates a small position, as the token may have some potential but may not be the most promising opportunity. A score of 78-84 suggests a medium position, indicating that the token has a good balance of risk and reward. A score of 85-92 indicates a large position, suggesting that the token has strong fundamentals and a high potential for growth.

### PHASE 3: TIMING DECISION

**Entry Timing:**
- [ ] **Pre-graduation:** Consider exiting 5-10% before graduation
- [ ] **Post-graduation:** Look for migration dip (30-50% common)
- [ ] **King of the Hill:** Approaching graduation? Accumulate
- [ ] **Narrative early:** Hot narrative, copycats not exploded? Buy leaders
- [ ] **Narrative rotation:** Money rotating to whole pack

The number can fluctuate based on market activity, new project launches, and the efficiency of the detection algorithm. A higher detection rate may indicate increased market participation, but it can also signal a higher risk of encountering scams or low-quality projects. For example, during a token sale season, this number might spike to 500 pairs per hour (illustrative), requiring enhanced filtering mechanisms.

- [ ] Number of pairs that pass 8-step check  
  The 8-step check is a multi-stage vetting process that evaluates various aspects of a token pair, including on-chain activity, liquidity, transaction patterns, and smart contract audits. Pairs that pass this check are considered more likely to be legitimate and less prone to rug pulls or other malicious activities. A typical pass rate might be around 30% (illustrative), depending on the market's overall health and the sophistication of the detection system.

A candidates list might include anywhere from 500 to 2,000 pairs (illustrative) at any given time.

## Advanced Strategies

### Strategy 1: Pre-Graduation Exit
**Thesis:** Sell just before graduation for more SOL
**Execution:**
- Monitor bonding curve progress: Track the percentage of tokens that have been sold on the bonding curve. Graduation occurs when a predetermined number of tokens (often 100 million or more) have been sold, which typically triggers a price increase due to reduced supply.
- Exit 5-10% before graduation threshold: If the bonding curve is expected to graduate at 100 million tokens sold, exit when around 95 million have been sold. This allows capture of the price increase without exposure to the volatility that often accompanies the graduation event.
- Don't wait for migration: Migration is the process where the token moves from the bonding curve to a new chain or protocol. This event can be highly volatile and may not always result in a price increase. Exiting before migration avoids this uncertainty.
- Price action often weakens as graduation approaches: As the graduation event nears, market participants may sell ahead of the event, leading to a temporary price dip. This can be exploited by selling at a lower price and re-entering after the price recovers.

For example, if a token is expected to graduate at 100 million tokens sold and the current price is $0.01, selling 5 million tokens before graduation might allow capturing a price increase to $0.012, resulting in a 20% profit. This strategy is particularly effective in tokens with a large bonding curve and high trading volume.

### Strategy 2: Migration Dip Buy
**Thesis:** Many tokens dump 30-50% immediately post-migration
**Execution:**
- Wait for migration to complete: Migration is the process of moving the token to a new blockchain or protocol. This can be a volatile event, and it is important to wait for the migration to be fully completed before considering a purchase.
- Look for 30-50% dip: After migration, the token may experience a significant price drop, often due to uncertainty or a lack of liquidity. A 30-50% dip is a common occurrence and represents an opportunity to buy at a lower price.
- Buy if fundamentals are strong: Fundamental analysis includes assessing the token’s utility, team, roadmap, and market demand. A strong fundamental base increases the likelihood of a price rebound post-dip.
- Lower risk than sniping, still early: Sniping involves buying a token immediately after its launch, which is highly volatile and risky. In contrast, buying after a migration dip is less risky but still offers early entry potential.

For example, if a token’s price drops from $0.02 to $0.01 immediately after migration, buying at $0.01 with a strong fundamental outlook could lead to a 100% profit if the price rebounds to $0.02.

### Strategy 3: Narrative Early
**Thesis:** Hot narratives where copycat launches have not exploded yet
**Execution:**
- Identify narratives just heating up: A narrative

## Common Mistakes to Avoid

### 1. Waiting for Graduation
**Problem:** Selling just before graduation yields more SOL  
**Solution:** Exit 5-10% before graduation threshold  

This strategy is based on the observation that tokens often experience a sharp decline in value immediately following the graduation event due to reduced demand from liquidity providers and decreased speculative interest. By exiting 5-10% before the graduation threshold, traders can lock in profits before the inevitable price drop. For example, if a token is expected to graduate at a supply level of 100,000 tokens, exiting at 95,000 or 90,000 can capture the peak price before the drop. However, traders must be cautious of false graduation signals or delayed graduations, which may require adjusting the exit point accordingly. It is also important to consider the token's historical behavior around graduation events to determine the optimal exit window.

### 2. Buying Post-Migration High
**Problem:** Many tokens dump 30-50% immediately post-migration  
**Solution:** Wait for migration dip, buy the dip  

Migration events, such as token swaps or network upgrades, often lead to significant price volatility. Historical data shows that tokens frequently experience a sharp price decline of 30-50% in the days following migration due to uncertainty, liquidity issues, or token holder behavior. Instead of buying at the peak just after migration, traders should wait for a dip that typically occurs within the first few days post-migration. This dip can be identified by monitoring price action, volume spikes, and sentiment analysis. For instance, a token that rises to $0.50 immediately after migration might fall to $0.35 within 24 hours, presenting a buying opportunity. However, traders must be wary of tokens with low liquidity or those that fail to meet migration criteria, which may result in a deeper or prolonged

## Quick Reference: Fresh Pair Trading Checklist

### Before Entry
- [ ] Contract security verified: Ensure that the smart contract has been audited by a reputable firm and has no known vulnerabilities. This is crucial to avoid rug pulls or exploits that could result in loss of funds. A verified contract is the first line of defense in memecoin trading.
- [ ] Holder distribution healthy: A healthy holder distribution indicates that the token is not concentrated in a few large wallets, which can be a red flag for potential dumping or manipulation. A more distributed holder base is generally considered more stable and less prone to sudden price swings.
- [ ] Social sentiment positive: Monitoring social media, forums, and chat platforms like Telegram and Discord can provide insights into community perception. Positive sentiment often correlates with price appreciation, though it's important to differentiate between genuine interest and hype.
- [ ] Price action healthy: Analyze the price chart for signs of healthy volatility, volume patterns, and support/resistance levels. Avoid entering a trade during a downtrend or a consolidation phase unless there are strong catalysts for a breakout.
- [ ] Community active/organic: A truly active and organic community is essential for the long-term success of a memecoin. Check for regular updates, active discussions, and a growing user base that is not just speculative but also engaged with the project's vision.
- [ ] Bonding curve progress checked: The bonding curve is a mechanism that determines how new tokens are issued and priced based on the amount of liquidity provided. Checking the bonding curve's progress can give insight into how much liquidity is already in the pool and how it might affect future price movements.
- [ ] Social sentiment scored: Assign a score to the social sentiment based on metrics like engagement rate, positive versus negative mentions, and the presence of influential figures endorsing the coin. A higher score may indicate a stronger potential for price appreciation.
- [ ] Narrative identified: A clear and compelling narrative is essential for a memecoin to gain traction. Whether it's based on humor, a social cause

## Summary

### Detection
- Pull new pairs every 60 seconds: This ensures traders are continuously updated on the latest memecoins entering the market. It allows for real-time monitoring of potential opportunities and risks. New pairs may emerge from various sources such as decentralized exchanges (DEXs) or social media platforms, and checking them every minute ensures traders do not miss any new developments.
- Use DexScreener, Pump.fun King of Hill: These tools are commonly used for identifying memecoins that may be pumping in value. DexScreener provides detailed analytics on token performance, while Pump.fun King of Hill highlights tokens that are experiencing rapid price increases. These platforms are crucial for detecting tokens that may be in the early stages of a price surge.
- Track bonding curve progress: Bonding curves are often used in memecoins to control the supply of tokens. By tracking the progress of a bonding curve, traders can understand the token's supply dynamics and how it might affect price volatility. A bonding curve that is nearing its capacity may signal a potential price increase or a possible rug pull.
- Monitor social sentiment: Social media platforms such as Twitter, Reddit, and Telegram are often where memecoins gain traction. Monitoring social sentiment helps traders gauge the community's interest and potential price movements. A surge in positive sentiment may indicate a possible price increase, while a sudden drop in sentiment may signal a potential sell-off.
- Identify narrative themes: Memecoins often have a unique story or theme that drives their value. Identifying these themes helps traders understand the underlying factors that may influence the token's performance. For example, a memecoin based on a popular internet meme may have a different narrative than one tied to a specific project or technology.
- Check wallet clusters: Wallet clusters can provide insight into the distribution of tokens among different holders. If a large number of tokens are concentrated in a few wallets, it may indicate a potential risk of a rug pull or manipulation. Traders should be cautious of tokens with a high concentration of tokens in a small number of wallets.
- Monitor smart money convergence: Smart money, such as large investors or institutional players, often move in tandem during market events. Monitoring their convergence can provide insight into potential price movements. If multiple large wallets begin to accumulate a particular token, it may signal a potential price increase.

### Analysis
- 8-step rug check (1 minute each): A rug check is a process used to determine whether a memecoin is a potential scam or rug pull.
